# LEDGER.md — the novel-results ledger of the Hadwiger–Nelson workspace

*Extracted from `CLAUDE.md` § Frontier on 2026-09-22 (Kooshi), so that what is looked up by address lives in a file and what is
reasoned from stays in context. **This file is not imported into `CLAUDE.md`; it is read at two moments** — the re-grokking
(WORKFLOW 2.0 step 3.1, every row's mechanism walked from inside the milestone's question, `CLAUDE.md` and this file open side by
side) and the close (step 9, the new row written here, "where the problem stands" rewritten there) — and consulted by row number
whenever a brief names a kernel consumer or a control value (a brief cites the row; the implementer reads the row, or better the
Lean module it names). Grades are `CLAUDE.md` § Notation's; the one-home-per-fact rule puts numbers, certificates, names and
addresses HERE and mechanism and readings in the grokkings, each pointing at the other; `tools/claude_md_fact_audit.py` audits
`CLAUDE.md` and this file as one corpus. Current state only, like `CLAUDE.md`: a corrected row is rewritten in place and the story
goes to git.*

*The 2026-09-25 crunch's three milestones are folded in: rows 98–102 (M1, FS-91), 103–107 (M2, the kernel lane) and 108–110
(M3), then row 88's question (rows 111–113) and FS-67 (rows 114–115), closed later the same day; its index is `plans/CRUNCH-2026-09-25-INDEX.md` and its items `plans/FS91-close-inventory.md`, `plans/M2-close-inventory.md` and
`plans/M3-close-inventory.md`.*

## The claim list — what is ours beyond Theorems A and B (moved from `CLAUDE.md` § The prior-art position, 2026-09-26)

*`CLAUDE.md` keeps the position's lineage sentence, Theorems A and B with `[Fischer1994]`'s value, every kernel certificate, and the whole
**Not ours** list, which stays in context against misclaims; this is the rest of its **Ours** list, verbatim, beside the rows it names.*

**Ours:** the sieve; the lift theory; the compulsion transfer and distance law; the tightness mechanism;
the fan-gap transport and its compulsion consumers (row 66); the tight/slack classifier of a residue plane's
compulsions and its propagation up a tower (row 67, G54); plane freedom at every prime p ≡ 3 (mod 4); the field-internal reading of
the fractional blow-up and `χ_f(K²) = 4` (row 71); the 3-adic barrier and the four-colour lower bounds for real quadratic
fields (rows 72–73); the fibre-share inequality and the all-levels certificates, with the answer to the `p`-adic Hadwiger–Nelson question (row 75); the forced group of de Grey's lattice (the lattice form of Hubai's 2018 ring computation) and its census; the three-colour criterion, density and dichotomy for abelian Cayley graphs and the `Θ` certificate (rows 78–79); the classification of four-colour edge-local heights over every generating set, the four no-gos by format and the rank-1 one-way walls and criterion, and `L21cc6506`'s looseness at four colours (rows 80–81); the passage from additive to all three-colourings at the field level and the census's structural certificates (rows 82–83; as far as the gate reached, `[Johnson2000]` and `[Johnson2006]` unread); `F = P = 8Λ` on de Grey's lattice by derivation and the audit of its input, the triangle-free abstract rigidity and its Soifer calibration, and the no-gos on `Λ` (rows 85–87); the type-(B) frame, the classification of `K²`'s four-colourings, the rigid local plane and the no-go family on type-(B) planes (rows 88–94); rational band rigidity at two or more primes, at every band, and its field consequences (rows 95–97, 98); the class theorem for character colourings, the character chromatic numbers, three colours over every real number field and one prime's three failures (rows 99–102); rank one at four colours reduced to single colourings, the mixing selection and the windows at five over ℚ(√167) (rows 108–110); the product criterion `χ(E_k(G)) ≤ k` for gap-free `G`, its `K_k × X` form and the sharp mixing pullback for every gap-free `G` (rows 114–115; as far as the gate reached, Duffus–Sands–Woodrow 1985 unread); prime inversion in valuation form, with no number field (row 105); four-colour rigidity split into invariant measures and transience, Theorem C over the real S2 fields and its ℚ(√47) form (rows 111–113); Kronecker selection refuted as stated, its reduction and its corrected form's reduction to one density step (row 116); T-C(A)'s class among real quadratic fields and SP-Cor over it (row 117); rank one of the selection and the non-irreducibility of the unit-vector group on its solenoid (row 118); the winding form of the three-colour criterion's ⟹ as a `Θ₃` certificate's semantics, and the certificate's kernel checker (row 119; as far as the gate reached; its ingredients are in print); the entropy split of measure rigidity for the unit-vector group and its Einsiedler–Fish transfer, and `χ(ℚ(√47)²) = 5` (rows 120–122); the class theorem and SP-Cor in every degree, so the local–global conjecture's main clause through five over every real number field, `χ(ℚ(√3,√5)²) = 5`, the type-(B) fields' rotation-invariant half and the reduction of six at ℚ(√167) to `χ_CP(ℝ²) ≥ 6` (rows 123–125) — as far as the gate reached (X24R: not found in print), `[Johnson2000]` and `[Johnson2006]` unread; TR-ROT's equivalence with Q-μ and its spectral closure on `E` (row 126; prior art not searched); the kernel form of the main clause through five over named hypotheses, with `χ(ℚ(√47)²) ≤ 5` unconditional (row 127; the upper half is row 52's colouring).

## A. The novel-results ledger

### The novel-results ledger

*Kernel-checked unless marked (grades: § Notation). Row numbers are stable identifiers cited across `plans/`: never renumber, never reuse. Lean modules
are under `Proofs/HadwigerNelson/`. Paths under `build/` are local, untracked, regenerable measurement
artifacts — provenance, never a certificate; the certificate is the Lean module.*

| # | Result | Where | When |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Two-circle theorem**: every unit-distance graph on the circles of radius 1 and 1/√3 about one point (the full uncountable set) is 3-colourable. | `TwoCircle.lean` (`colorable_three_induce`) | M19 |
| 2 | **Seam identity**: the cross-chord rotation between those circles is σ = (√3 + i√33)/6, of infinite order; two-circle labels form a strip ℤ/6 × ℤ. | `TwoCircle.seam_pow_ne_one` | M19 |
| 3 | **Coverage ratchet**: `CoverAtLeast` is monotone under adding edges, widening the class and lowering the level; at level k it is the fan. | `CoverageMono.lean` | M19 |
| 4 | **Fan ⟺ compulsory coverage**: a pivot is fanned iff every proper k-colouring puts all k colours on N(p) ∪ Q. | `Coverage.lean` (`fan_iff_coverage`) | M18 |
| 5 | **No class confines alone**: in-class degree ≤ 2, so class-only graphs are 3-colourable and a fourth colour frees the pivot. | `ClassGeometry.lean` (`not_fan_classOnlyGraph`) | M18 |
| 6 | **120° triangle identity**: a unit triangle lies on a circle about p iff r² = 1/3, and then p is its centroid. | `ClassGeometry.lean` (`exists_unit_triangle_iff`) | M18 |
| 7 | **Greedy colouring** (degree < k everywhere ⟹ k-colourable), absent from Mathlib. | `Greedy.lean` | M18 |

| 79 | **Three colours are linear: every Cayley graph of a finitely generated torsion-free abelian group is 3-colourable iff a character puts every generator in `[1/3, 2/3]`, its periodic 3-colourings are dense, and forced pairs are exactly what periodic colourings see** (FS-73's competition; the theorems `plans/FS73-derivation.md` T21–T30, T39–T45; the understanding is G71). **[hand; two independent derivations — de Bruijn and Coxeter — read by Poros; the owner checked the two deciding steps]** For `Λ` finitely generated torsion-free and `S = −S` finite generating: **(criterion)** `Cay(Λ, S)` is 3-colourable ⟺ `Θ₃ = {θ ∈ Hom(Λ, ℝ/ℤ) : θ(S) ⊆ [1/3, 2/3]} ≠ ∅` ⟺ it has a periodic (circular) 3-colouring — every 3-colouring has an integer height through `K₃`'s universal cover whose winding is realised by a point of `Θ₃`; **(density)** periodic 3-colourings are dense, so **`P = F` (C3-inherit) and C3-aper hold at three colours for the whole class, with no arithmetic of the lattice**; **(dichotomy)** `F` has finite index ⟺ the colouring space is finite ⟺ every cell of `Θ₃` is a point, and if every point has order 3 every 3-colouring is affine. This answers `[AbrishamiEtAl2025]` Problem 4.6 at `χ = 3` for these graphs, and reproduces `[FourUnitVectors2025]` Thm 1.2 in one line. **[tools, verified + exact] ℚ(√11)'s `Λ₁₀`** (the 36 unit vectors of denominator dividing 10): **`F = P = 3Λ`**, first by FS-58's two forcers (52 and 65 points, both needed) and ten separators (independently verified: drat-trim and exhaustive enumeration), then **derived with no forcer and no solver**: `Θ₃(Λ₁₀)` is exactly 16 isolated points of order 3, so all 48 three-colourings are affine (one orbit) and `F = ⋂ ker φ = 3Λ`; the 100-vector `Λ₅₀` has `F = P = 3Λ₅₀` (replicated, not independently verified). **[cert] The `Θ`-emptiness certificate** — an exact tree with Farkas leaves and a Fraction-only checker that re-enumerates the lattice's unit vectors from its key: FS-61's ℚ(√47) shape is not 3-colourable in 2,997 nodes (653 KB) against a refusing ball's LRAT of tens of MB; `Θ₃` agrees with the solver record on nine lattices and with a transfer-matrix decider on 2,299 rank-1 sets. **ℚ(√167)** (the conjecture's cheapest exposed prediction, ≥ 6, against a proven 3 before this row): **[cert]** `L3e1c94c6` — P2's "no periodic colouring" — has a gapped residue colouring of period 23 at `(23, √167 − 11)`, its 36 unit vectors reaching half the residue circle; 52 of FS-61's 57 lattices carry certified gapped periodic three-colourings and 3 are 3-integral, so **FS-61's null at 167 is explained** — its one ask that could refuse (the one lattice reaching degree 14 under a 2M-point cap, 1,474,541 points, three-coloured in 3.66 s) is a noisy circular colouring (T20), and its reading that 167's 3-non-integral lattices hold too few unit vectors (20–36 against 11's 108) held for the lattices it asked, while its two richest (44 and 60 unit vectors) are the two the screens left open; the residue sets at 23 that colour are classified (224 classes, 98 colour, every one with a gap; the smallest refusing set a `μ₆` coset) and at 43 need ≥ 5 pairs to refuse — a design rule; **[exact]** `Ld14c588b` is 3-colourable by a linear form of period index 1,080 (Coxeter; the owner verified), past every period the searches reached; **[cert + hand] and `L21cc6506` (60 unit vectors, denominator 420) has `Θ₃ = ∅`, so it is not 3-colourable and `χ(ℚ(√167)²) ≥ 4`** — the first bound above three where the conjecture predicts six (FS-59's first target; at four colours `L21cc6506` is four-colourable and loose, row 81) — by two independent exact certificates: implementer E's two routes (a θ-space tree of 605,203 nodes, 7.7 MB gzipped, accepted by Coxeter's unmodified checker and by E's own; and a structural certificate over the product `U = {1, v, v̄, z, z̄}·U_R`, 4,768 Farkas-refuted cases, 106 KB, with its own standalone checker) and verifier V2's relation-branching tree (written before reading E: 30,344 leaves over 39 relations, 149 KB gzipped, `data/fs73/verify-v2/L21cc6506-empty.json.gz`, a second structurally different tree also passing), each checked by a Fraction/int-only checker that re-enumerates the lattice's unit vectors (`tools/fs73v2_check.py`: the owner re-ran it, PASS, and five planted faults were each refused for its own reason; a fake-empty certificate for a colourable lattice is refused), with the criterion's ⟹ direction (T22) derived independently by both competitors, read by Poros and the owner, and re-derived a third way by V2 (a forward `{1, 2}` lift: no parity step, no generation hypothesis). **A half-page proof exists** (E, checked line by line by V2): the 36 unit vectors `{1, v, z}·U_R` alone have empty `Θ₃` — a minimal obstruction — by four inequality cases. **No finite non-3-colourable graph is exhibited**; | 80 | **Four colours, cell by cell: no certificate of non-four-colourability comes from the colour complex one cell at a time — edge-local heights exist only on four-colourable presentations of rank ≤ 2, the loop winding is the graph's parity, parity-free face fluxes are trivial, coindex topological bounds stop at four on unit-distance graphs — and plain density of periodic four-colourings fails in rank 1** (FS-79's competition and plan; the theorems `plans/FS79-derivation.md` T3–T7, T17–T37; the understanding is G73). **The class** 𝒞: Cayley graphs of finitely generated torsion-free abelian groups, `S = −S` finite, taken in the canonical form `Λ = ⟨S⟩` (T6). **[hand; two independent derivations, de Bruijn's and Coxeter's, each key step read by Poros; exact rank checks with plants; the owner checked three steps] Four no-gos, each for its format:** (1) **edge-local heights**, in the format the advisor pinned (closed on commutator squares; winding and slope as the certificate's data; no window larger than one square tested): a non-trivial four-colour edge-local height exists **iff `S` has a sum-closed half** — `±{a}`, `±{a, 2a}` or `±{a, b, a+b}`, rank ≤ 2 — where it is Moore–Newman's height generalised, and every such graph is four-colourable, so **the format certifies non-four-colourability on no graph of 𝒞** (de Bruijn's C1 with Lemmas D and S, T18–T21; Coxeter's square lemma reached the triangle-free case independently, T24); among unit-distance lattices of rank ≥ 2 only ℤ[ζ₆] carries one; at `k ≥ 5` colours no presentation with two or more generator classes carries one, because the Kneser graph `KG(k, 2)` is connected, and at `k = 4` the extra space is exactly two-dimensional — `K₄`'s three perfect matchings, `V₄` (T22); on the colouring shift's own language the same holds under two occurrence conditions — exactly on all 9,120 of de Grey's four-colourings and on `L21cc6506`'s exhibited ones (T23, T25), so **de Grey's rigidity (`F = 8Λ`, row 78) is not an edge-local height**; (2) **the loop winding** FS73-T29 named is each relation's length parity, the same for every colouring (`Q₃ = K₄ × K₂`, T17; Coxeter, and Poros independently); (3) **face-local 2-forms, one dimension up**: 24 of `Q₃`'s 2,652 four-colourings are monopoles and kill the skyrmion class locally (T27); in the parity-free format, the only one a non-bipartite graph admits, every closed form is `dη + κ` wherever at least three directions span induced squares and 3-cubes — every triangle-free unit-distance lattice, by the rhombus lemma (T28–T29, with Poros's `n ≥ 3` repair) — and the signed flux is slaved to the charges on every non-bipartite graph (T30); (4) **the coindex topological bounds** `coind(B₀) + 1`, `coind(H) + 2`, `conn(N) + 3` are at most 4 on every K₂,₃-free graph, so on every unit-distance graph, by `[SimonyiTardos2006]`'s Zig-zag Theorem (T26). **[tools, three independent checks + hand] Rank 1:** plain density of periodic four-colourings is **false** — FS73-T30's four walls are one-way (T3) — while existence and `P = F` hold there; the invariant-measure LP on windows of length `max D + 1` is exact, so window frequencies are not controlled by `χ_f` (T4); `G(ℤ,{1,3,7,12,17,22})` refuses four colours on 33 points with `χ_f ≤ 29/8`, and a Cartesian product with ℤ makes a rank-2 one, so (Q-obs) asks for refutation rank `ρ₄ ≥ 2` (T5, T7); `P ≠ F` iff every cyclic phase is rigid and a wall exists (T35); a counterexample periodic in `r − 1` directions has two rigid ends, one chiral, never additive (T34); no counterexample among the 2,706 sets to `max D = 14`, `\|D\| ≤ 5` (T36). **De Grey's lattice:** each of its 48 non-Eisenstein unit vectors lies alone in an Eisenstein coset, so the stripes and `F = 8Λ` are a many-layer effect (T37). **Predictions** (`plans/FS79-invariant-question.md` §P, scored in §R-close.5): P-B′ (a no-go of a named kind, 0.18) won; the parity reading of the winding; the face-local no-go and its gluing; the one-way walls and the exact rank-1 criterion; the chirality reduction as a theorem. **Not claimed:** cocycles reading windows of radius ≥ 2 (`[CGHO2025coh]` Thm 5.8's gluing route is open for 𝒞), the **index** topological bounds `ind(B₀) + 1` and `ind(H) + 2`, non-local 2-forms, window-frequency LPs in rank ≥ 2, algebraic or counting certificates; | 85 | **Four colours on de Grey's lattice, derived: every proper four-colouring of `Cay(Λ, U)` is `8Λ`-periodic, local at one prime above 2 (the chirality), pair-linear and striped on every Eisenstein coset; `F = P = 8Λ`; exactly 9,120 — from Parts's trees, with no solver refusal of ours** (FS-85; the theorems `plans/FS85-derivation.md` FS85-T0, dB5–dB11, V6–V8, IA1–IA4, V2-T1, C7–C15, R3a, R3b, V2-T5, V2-T8; the understanding is G54, G69, G70, G75). **[hand + tools exact, given (S″); (S″) checked here in full]** (S″) is `[Parts2020hv]`'s theorem that no four-colouring has a monochromatic √3-triple (his 481-vertex G481 and nine trees, found by his program), placed in `Λ = (1/9)·O_E` in the five inner orientations `η^k`, `\|k\| ≤ 2` (V8: exact; `η^{±3}`, `η^{±4}` copies leave `Λ` at 150 and 342 points). **The input, audited (IA1–IA4):** all nine trees walked as a fixed certificate from a by-eye transcription (880 cells of L1–L8; Tables 2 and 3 reproduced exactly; one plant per tree refused), the root case split (36 colourings) proved exhaustive by hand, each root reached under a named symmetry. **The chain (two independent derivations, R3b = V2-T7):** the spectral lemma (C7: NT_k is the linear identity `A_k g = −2g`, symbol `(1+X)(1+Y)(X+Y)/XY`, triangles entering through `1 + ζ₆² = ζ₆`) pins the spectrum to `Θ_K`; exact tables (C8: 42 characters for all nine families, the dual of `8Λ`; 7,290 for `\|k\| ≤ 2`, period `120Λ`) give **`F ⊇ 120Λ`, `P = F` from G481 alone** (C10); the 5-part lemma (R3a, conditional form: NT for `\|k\| ≤ 2` makes every five-family component local at one prime, so `24Λ`) and the alternate-triple lemma (C15: odd-index cosets carry NT to families ±3, ±4) give NT in all nine families; Theorem R (C9) collapses every colouring onto striped colourings of the 64-vertex torus `Cay(T₀/8T₀, μ₆ ∪ 3μ₆)` pulled back along one prime, and **counts 9,120 with no enumeration** (`2 × (3·1,536 − 3·24 + 24)`). Derived by Coxeter (C7–C15) and the round-3 agent (R3a–R3b), then by verifier 2 blind (W, V2-T1–T7): no divergence of truth, twelve of scope, notation and route. **A second mechanism for `P = F`** (V7: de Bruijn's dB9–dB11 and verifier 1, blind): adjacent striped cosets share a direction unless one is residue-type, one residue-type coset forces the global residue colouring, so every colouring is periodic under one of 243 finite-index lattices, **`F ⊇ 1680Λ`**. So `P = F` on `Λ` holds by three mechanisms: FS-73's certificate (row 78), stripe coupling, and the spectrum. **Given 8Λ-periodicity** (round 1, dB5–dB8): `Cay(Λ/8Λ, U)` is exactly Hoffman-tight at four colours (`λ_min = −12`, 42 characters split 21 + 21 by prime), so every `8Λ`-periodic colouring is local at one prime (dB6), Hubai's chirality is the choice of prime (dB7: `η² ≡ ζ₃^{±1}`), and the 64-vertex dyadic plane's colourings are exactly stripes (dB8, by the non-existence of a complete mapping of `ℤ/4`). **A published proof corrected (V6):** the written proof of `[Polymath16wikiR2]` Prop. 7 part 1 does not close (a slip, `ρ_{2η²} = 0` for 1, and a dead last step; a 2,475-point object built by formula satisfies every inequality it lists); on `Λ`, (S″) closes it in one line. **Prior art and credit:** the value and the count are `[Hubai2018]`'s (the ring as his computation uses it, by computer) and FS-73's (row 78, by certificate); | 100 | **The class theorem for character colourings (CLG), at existence scope: for every real number field `F` and `k ≥ 2`, given band rigidity (row 98), the plane has a band `k`-character — equivalently a character `k`-colouring `x ↦ ⌊kθ(x)⌋` — iff some stable place of `F(i)` carries one at some level of its residue tower; so `χ_char(F²) = min` over the residue targets, and at `k ≤ 5`, and at every `k` over fields of degree ≤ 2, a character `k`-colouring exists only if `L(F) ≤ k`** (FS-91; FS91-J07-1 to J07-15, J10-L1, J10-L2, J10-L7, J10-L8, J11-T12). **[hand (+ tools exact); two independent derivations (J07; J10 blind in phase 1, then from J07's 25 lemma statements: 24 TRUE, one GAP)]** The peel: by row 98, `θ = τ + α`; the archimedean part vanishes at every complex place and is an interval about 0 at each real one, so `τ` is band; `τ` factors through finitely many stable places (Lemma C: `R ⊗ ℤ_p = R̄_p × V_n`; Lemma C′: `R_F = O′`, the ring integral at the stable primes, with **no Galois hypothesis**, except possibly at a ramified stable dyadic place — three derivations; the exception occurs at ℚ(√5), ℚ(√17), ℚ(α)); weak approximation makes the image of `U` a product of local images; and a sum of symmetric subsets of the circle inside the band keeps **some** summand inside it (the peeling lemma, three derivations). Given MR, over ℚ(√59), ℚ(√83), ℚ(√131) row 124's one step TR-ROT is equivalent to Q-μ for translation-invariant measures, and every translation-invariant random four-colouring's spectral measure gives `E ∖ T₄` mass 0 (`T₄` the characters trivial on `R ∩ 𝔓⁴`, a finite group; torsion and archimedean orbits never approach it, and a split-place component goes to Haar under a hyperbolic shift), so T-C′'s (R1) is closed: a colouring measure whose spectrum is carried by `E` is a pullback. What is left is one wall about the algebraic action `U ↷ R̂` alone, NA₀: "No nonzero finite positive `E`-null measure on `R̂` is `T₄`-attracted under `U`" (every `U`-Følner average of it converging onto `T₄`), which given MR implies TR-ROT** (FS-100 stage 5, wave 3b; FS100-X27D-EQ, FS100-X27D-E; `plans/FS100-three-field-lemma.md` §R-close (stage 5, wave 3b)). **[hand; the equivalence, the closure on `E` and the flip's equivalence two derivations blind to each other (X27D; X27V, blind verdicts committed at `9feb1e73`), given MR (row 120) and X22D-QR; the counterexample's shape one mind, its first two clauses matched blind]** A counterexample — row 88's "no" — has an `E`-null, Haar-singular spectral remainder that every rotation average carries onto `T₄`, over an exactly `𝔓⁴`-periodic conditional colour law (FS100-X27D-ATT); X27V's COL-E is the wall's exact form given MR, NA₀ a sufficient one. The flip — a quantity zero on the pullbacks and positive on every non-pullback measure — is vacuous as posed and, in the kind an averaging proof uses, equivalent to TR-ROT: retired (FS100-X27D-FL). Closed routes: two-summand combinatorics (a Sierpiński decomposition; FS100-X27D-SIERP) and points to measures (FS100-X27V-PM). **Not claimed:** TR-ROT; NA₀; COL-E; row 88. **Prior art:** not searched; NA₀'s home is homoclinic points of algebraic actions (`[LindSchmidt1999]`, reviews only, unread). | `plans/FS100-derivation.md` §5; `plans/FS100-X27D-derivation.md` §2–§3, Stage 4; `plans/FS100-X27V-verification.md` §2–§4; `tools/x27d_ramanujan.py` | FS-100 stage 5, wave 3b (crunch 2026-09-26) |

## B. Theorems proved outside the kernel, by milestone

*One line per theorem a milestone stated and proved by hand, by derivation from held results, or at tools grade — the
intermediate theorems that the rows above compress into prose. Each milestone's `plans/FSnn-derivation.md` holds the full entry
(statement, grade, proof's address, what rests on it, kernel status); this table is the index. Minted 2026-09-22 (Kooshi); backfilled for M17–FS-66 by
the theorem audit (FS-71, row 77) the same day — 254 entries in 53 files, `tools/fs71_ledger_b_check.py --strict` keeps the two in step.*
